{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_01PdomoW5jzzUsjDDacSLbCw","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01KopqzpxNbXhu9tvqU2mrzv","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/openpyxl-excel-manipulation.md","content":"# OpenPyXL Excel Manipulation Skill\n\n## Overview\nOpenPyXL is a Python library for reading and writing Excel 2010 xlsx/xlsm/xltx/xltm files. This skill covers common operations for manipulating Excel workbooks.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install openpyxl\n```\n\n## Basic Operations\n\n### Loading a Workbook\n```python\nfrom openpyxl import load_workbook\n\n# Load existing workbook\nwb = load_workbook('file.xlsx')\n\n# Access sheets\nsheet_names = wb.sheetnames  # List of sheet names\nws = wb['SheetName']  # Access specific sheet\nws = wb.active  # Access active sheet\n```\n\n### Creating a New Workbook\n```python\nfrom openpyxl import Workbook\n\nwb = Workbook()\nws = wb.active\nws.title = \"MySheet\"\n```\n\n### Reading Cell Values\n```python\n# Single cell\nvalue = ws['A1'].value\nvalue = ws.cell(row=1, column=1).value\n\n# Range of cells\nfor row in ws.iter_rows(min_row=1, max_row=10, min_col=1, max_col=5):\n    for cell in row:\n        print(cell.value)\n\n# Get all values from a column\ncol_values = [cell.value for cell in ws['A']]\n```\n\n### Writing Cell Values\n```python\n# Direct assignment\nws['A1'] = \"Hello\"\nws.cell(row=1, column=1, value=\"Hello\")\n\n# Write formulas\nws['B1'] = \"=A1*2\"\nws['C1'] = \"=SUM(A1:A10)\"\n```\n\n### Excel Formulas\n```python\n# Common formulas\nws['A1'] = \"=LN(B1/C1)*100\"  # Natural log, multiply by 100 for percentage\nws['A2'] = \"=STDEV(A1:A12)\"  # Standard deviation\nws['A3'] = \"=SQRT(12)*A2\"   # Annualized volatility\nws['A4'] = \"=AVERAGE(A1:A10)\"  # Average\nws['A5'] = '=XLOOKUP(A1,B:B,C:C)'  # XLOOKUP\nws['A6'] = '=INDEX(A:A,MATCH(B1,C:C,0))'  # INDEX+MATCH\n```\n\n### Saving Workbook\n```python\nwb.save('output.xlsx')\n```\n\n## Working with Multiple Sheets\n```python\n# Create new sheet\nws_new = wb.create_sheet(\"NewSheet\")\n\n# Copy data between sheets\nfor row in ws1.iter_rows():\n    for cell in row:\n        ws2[cell.coordinate].value = cell.value\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Always close/save workbooks after modifications\n2. Use `data_only=True` when loading if you only need values (not formulas)\n3. Be careful with formula syntax - Excel uses comma separators in functions\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":416,"cache_read_input_tokens":19208,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":416,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c0b58a7f-44bf-4ee0-a966-4e71c5b5403e","uuid":"f8b32c66-77ba-4e21-a216-038e77f003be"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_01UDsrajPahKf7uQjn1L5dE4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01HtobzhjAgoezhhHf9aQ8Zr","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/financial-volatility-calculations.md","content":"# Financial Volatility Calculations Skill\n\n## Overview\nThis skill covers calculating log returns and volatility for financial time series data, commonly used in risk analysis.\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### Log Returns\nLog returns (also called continuously compounded returns) are calculated as:\n```\nLog Return = ln(P_t / P_{t-1}) * 100  (expressed as percentage)\n```\n\nWhere:\n- P_t = Price at time t\n- P_{t-1} = Price at previous time period\n- ln = Natural logarithm\n\n### Volatility\nVolatility is the standard deviation of returns over a period.\n\n**N-month rolling volatility:**\n```\nVolatility_N = STDEV(returns over N months)\n```\n\n### Annualized Volatility\nTo annualize monthly volatility:\n```\nAnnualized Volatility = Monthly Volatility * sqrt(12)\n```\n\nFor 3-month volatility annualized:\n```\nAnnualized_3M = 3-month STDEV * sqrt(12)\n```\n\n## Excel Formulas\n\n### Monthly Log Return (as percentage)\n```excel\n=LN(B2/B3)*100\n```\nWhere B2 is current month price, B3 is previous month price.\n\n### 3-Month Volatility\n```excel\n=STDEV(C2:C4)\n```\nWhere C2:C4 contains the last 3 monthly log returns.\n\n### 12-Month Volatility\n```excel\n=STDEV(C2:C13)\n```\nWhere C2:C13 contains the last 12 monthly log returns.\n\n### Annualized Volatility (from monthly)\n```excel\n=D2*SQRT(12)\n```\nWhere D2 is the monthly/N-month volatility.\n\n## Reserves at Risk (RaR)\n\nRaR measures potential loss in reserve value due to price volatility:\n```\nRaR = Gold Reserves Value * Volatility * Confidence Factor\n```\n\nFor a simple calculation:\n```\nRaR = Gold Reserves Value * Annualized Volatility\n```\n\n## Excel Implementation Example\n\n| Row | A (Date) | B (Price) | C (Log Return %) | D (3M Vol) | E (12M Vol) |\n|-----|----------|-----------|------------------|------------|-------------|\n| 2   | Jan-24   | 2000      | =LN(B2/B3)*100   | =STDEV(C2:C4) | =STDEV(C2:C13) |\n| 3   | Dec-23   | 1950      | =LN(B3/B4)*100   | =STDEV(C3:C5) | =STDEV(C3:C14) |\n\n## Notes\n- Always check data ordering (newest first vs oldest first)\n- Log returns are additive over time\n- Volatility scales with square root of time\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":884,"cache_read_input_tokens":19624,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":884,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c0b58a7f-44bf-4ee0-a966-4e71c5b5403e","uuid":"579e60e7-67b6-4b25-8c35-7949df28161c"}

This skill covers INDEX+MATCH and XLOOKUP.\n\n## XLOOKUP (Excel 365+)\nModern lookup function that replaces VLOOKUP.\n\n### Syntax\n```excel\n=XLOOKUP(lookup_value, lookup_array, return_array, [if_not_found], [match_mode], [search_mode])\n```\n\n### Examples\n```excel\n# Basic lookup\n=XLOOKUP(A1, 'Sheet2'!A:A, 'Sheet2'!B:B)\n\n# With default value if not found\n=XLOOKUP(A1, 'Sheet2'!A:A, 'Sheet2'!B:B, \"Not Found\")\n\n# Cross-sheet lookup for country data\n=XLOOKUP(B11, 'Total Reserves'!A:A, 'Total Reserves'!B:B, \"\")\n```\n\n## INDEX + MATCH\nClassic combination that's more flexible than VLOOKUP.\n\n### Syntax\n```excel\n=INDEX(return_range, MATCH(lookup_value, lookup_range, 0))\n```\n\n### Examples\n```excel\n# Basic lookup\n=INDEX(B:B, MATCH(A1, A:A, 0))\n\n# Cross-sheet lookup\n=INDEX('Total Reserves'!B:B, MATCH(A11, 'Total Reserves'!A:A, 0))\n\n# Two-way lookup (row and column)\n=INDEX(data_range, MATCH(row_lookup, row_headers, 0), MATCH(col_lookup, col_headers, 0))\n```\n\n### Match Types\n- 0: Exact match (most common)\n- 1: Less than (sorted ascending)\n- -1: Greater than (sorted descending)\n\n## Cross-Sheet References\nWhen referencing other sheets:\n```excel\n# Single quotes around sheet name (required if name has spaces)\n='Sheet Name'!A1\n\n# Range reference\n='Total Reserves'!A1:B100\n\n# In formulas\n=XLOOKUP(A1, 'Total Reserves'!A:A, 'Total Reserves'!C:C)\n```\n\n## OpenPyXL Implementation\n```python\n# Writing XLOOKUP formula\nws['A1'] = '=XLOOKUP(B1,\\'Other Sheet\\'!A:A,\\'Other Sheet\\'!B:B,\"\")'\n\n# Writing INDEX+MATCH formula\nws['A1'] = '=INDEX(\\'Total Reserves\\'!C:C,MATCH(B1,\\'Total Reserves\\'!A:A,0))'\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Use XLOOKUP for newer Excel versions (simpler syntax)\n2. Use INDEX+MATCH for compatibility with older Excel\n3. Always handle #N/A errors with IFERROR or XLOOKUP's if_not_found parameter\n4. Use absolute references ($) when copying formulas\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":583,"cache_read_input_tokens":21337,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":583,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c0b58a7f-44bf-4ee0-a966-4e71c5b5403e","uuid":"4a01e90c-7404-44bf-a749-a6f4d38d92a0"}

Use XLOOKUP for newer Excel versions (simpler syntax)\n2. Use INDEX+MATCH for compatibility with older Excel\n3. Always handle #N/A errors with IFERROR or XLOOKUP's if_not_found parameter\n4. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_01RCWqLTaNj2sJTXJfnHRoWv","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01KB4TRHAKKxcRMdXaXWieKE","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/reserves-at-risk-calculation.md","content":"# Reserves at Risk (RaR) Calculation Skill\n\n## Overview\nReserves at Risk (RaR) is a measure used to quantify potential losses in foreign exchange reserves due to price volatility, particularly for gold holdings.\n\n## Concept\nRaR captures the potential valuation swing in reserves based on:\n1. The value of gold reserves held\n2. The volatility of gold prices\n3. A time horizon (typically annualized)\n\n## Formula\n```\nRaR = Gold Reserves Value × Volatility (annualized)\n```\n\nOr with confidence level:\n```\nRaR = Gold Reserves Value × Volatility × Z-score\n```\n\nWhere Z-score = 1.645 for 95% confidence, 2.326 for 99% confidence.\n\n## Gold Price Exposure\nGold price exposure represents the potential near-term valuation impact:\n```\nGold Price Exposure = Gold Reserves Value × 3-month Annualized Volatility\n```\n\nThis captures short-term risk to reserve valuations.\n\n## Implementation in Excel\n\n### Step 1: Calculate Gold Reserves Value\n```excel\nGold Reserves Value = Volume (oz) × Gold Price ($/oz)\n```\n\n### Step 2: Get Volatility\nUse 3-month or 12-month volatility, annualized:\n```excel\nAnnualized Vol = Monthly Vol × SQRT(12)\n```\n\n### Step 3: Calculate RaR\n```excel\n=Gold_Value * Volatility\n```\n\n## Example Calculation\n| Country | Gold Value ($M) | Volatility (%) | RaR ($M) |\n|---------|-----------------|----------------|----------|\n| USA     | 500,000         | 15%            | 75,000   |\n| Germany | 200,000         | 15%            | 30,000   |\n\n```excel\nRaR = 500000 * 0.15 = 75000\n```\n\n## Data Requirements\n1. **Gold Reserves**:\n   - Can be in Value (USD) or Volume (troy ounces)\n   - If Volume, convert to Value using gold price\n\n2. **Gold Price**:\n   - Use appropriate period average (e.g., Jan-Sep for 2025 estimate)\n\n3. **Total Reserves**:\n   - Needed to contextualize gold holdings\n   - Use same year data for consistency\n\n## Notes\n- RaR is typically expressed as absolute value, not percentage\n- Higher volatility = Higher RaR = More risk\n- Countries with larger gold holdings have higher RaR\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":843,"cache_read_input_tokens":21920,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":843,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c0b58a7f-44bf-4ee0-a966-4e71c5b5403e","uuid":"ad623631-3a5d-4746-ac1d-0668c2dd8ab8"}

The value of gold reserves held\n2. The volatility of gold prices\n3. A time horizon (typically annualized)\n\n## Formula\n```\nRaR = Gold Reserves Value × Volatility (annualized)\n```\n\nOr with confidence level:\n```\nRaR = Gold Reserves Value × Volatility × Z-score\n```\n\nWhere Z-score = 1.645 for 95% confidence, 2.326 for 99% confidence.\n\n## Gold Price Exposure\nGold price exposure represents the potential near-term valuation impact:\n```\nGold Price Exposure = Gold Reserves Value × 3-month Annualized Volatility\n```\n\nThis captures short-term risk to reserve valuations.\n\n## Implementation in Excel\n\n### Step 1: Calculate Gold Reserves Value\n```excel\nGold Reserves Value = Volume (oz) × Gold Price ($/oz)\n```\n\n### Step 2: Get Volatility\nUse 3-month or 12-month volatility, annualized:\n```excel\nAnnualized Vol = Monthly Vol × SQRT(12)\n```\n\n### Step 3: Calculate RaR\n```excel\n=Gold_Value * Volatility\n```\n\n## Example Calculation\n| Country | Gold Value ($M) | Volatility (%) | RaR ($M) |\n|---------|-----------------|----------------|----------|\n| USA     | 500,000         | 15%            | 75,000   |\n| Germany | 200,000         | 15%            | 30,000   |\n\n```excel\nRaR = 500000 * 0.15 = 75000\n```\n\n## Data Requirements\n1. **Gold Reserves**:\n   - Can be in Value (USD) or Volume (troy ounces)\n   - If Volume, convert to Value using gold price\n\n2. **Gold Price**:\n   - Use appropriate period average (e.g., Jan-Sep for 2025 estimate)\n\n3. **Total Reserves**:\n   - Needed to contextualize gold holdings\n   - Use same year data for consistency\n\n## Notes\n- RaR is typically expressed as absolute value, not percentage\n- Higher volatility = Higher RaR = More risk\n- Countries with larger gold holdings have higher RaR\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
